# Tracker NOTES

Methodology decisions, source-conflict resolutions, and revision log. Bullets are append-only and dated. The git history of this file is the canonical audit trail.

- "Suspected Cases" includes all clinically suspected cases reported by DRC MoH; many are unconfirmed by lab testing.
- "Confirmed Cases" = laboratory-confirmed Bundibugyo virus by PCR at INRB or partner labs.
- Death counts are "suspected deaths" — most have not been lab-confirmed. True CFR is uncertain.
- Uganda confirmed cases stood at 2 (both imported from DRC) through May 22; on May 23 Uganda MoH confirmed 3 additional cases (a health worker, a driver linked to the index patient, and a returning Congolese traveler), bringing the total to 5. The health-worker case indicates probable secondary local transmission in Uganda.
- On May 21, CDC revised the May 20 total down from 675 to 626 as suspected cases were reclassified.
- Figures may differ slightly between sources due to reporting lag and retroactive reclassification.
- The outbreak was officially declared May 15; earliest known symptom onset was April 24, 2026.
- Sources: BNO News (bnonews.com), WHO Disease Outbreak News, CDC Ebola Situation Summary, Africa CDC, DRC MoH.
- 2026-05-23: May 21 row revised per WHO DON603 (data as of 21 May): suspected 671→746, confirmed 64→85, deaths 166→176. WHO's reconciled figures published the next day exceeded BNO News's same-day reporting.
- Column I 'Source Timestamp' shows the source's 'as of' date for each row. Each daily run re-checks the last 7 rows against the latest WHO/CDC/Africa CDC/BNO reports and applies revisions in place; revisions are logged here.

Old text: 'Uganda confirmed cases (2) are imported from DRC; no onward transmission reported in Uganda as of May 22.'

- 2026-05-24 (PM run): New row appended for 2026-05-24 based on DRC Ministry of Health statement released late Saturday 23 May (via AFP wire, carried by Citizen Digital and NPR/GPB on 24 May): 867 suspected cases and 204 suspected deaths across Ituri, Nord-Kivu, and Sud-Kivu provinces. - 2026-05-24 (scope change): Renamed main-sheet column headers from "(DRC+Uganda)" to "(Global)" to make the tracker country-agnostic. Historical values unchanged. Added a "Country Breakdown" sheet (long format: Date x Country x counts) pre-populated with derived DRC + Uganda rows. When a new country reports cases, add them to the global totals on the main sheet AND add per-country rows on the Country Breakdown sheet. NOTE: The American citizen who tested positive in DRC on May 17 and was medevaced to Germany is presumed counted in the DRC tally per WHO convention (country of exposure). If WHO begins splitting "in-country" vs "exported" cases, the breakdown sheet should track this explicitly.

Global confirmed: 87 → 96 (+10.3%). Suspected deaths held at 204 per DRC MoH; BNO additionally reports 10 confirmed deaths in DRC, kept out of col E to preserve the historical 'suspected deaths' definition and flagged here for transparency. Country Breakdown sheet records the 10 confirmed DRC deaths in col F. Uganda unchanged at 5 confirmed / 1 death. Outbreak now spans 12 health zones across Ituri, Nord-Kivu, Sud-Kivu provinces. BNO's headline 'more than 960 cases / 216 deaths' implies a +1 Uganda death not corroborated by Uganda MoH or CDC; treating as a BNO summary rounding pending confirmation. 7-day lookback against last 7 rows: no revisions required from higher-tier sources (no WHO Sitrep 02 published; latest WHO DON603 is data as of 21 May).

Uganda confirmed: 5 → 7. No new deaths; Uganda total stands at 7 confirmed / 1 death. These DRC numbers supersede the BNO News (May 25) figures (867 sus / 91 conf / 204 sus deaths) used in the AM run, per source-preference order (wire reports citing DRC government attribution > BNO News).

- 2026-05-25 (PM run, xcancel pass): BNO News @BNOFeed (citing RFI), corroborated by another wire-style account, reports that 7 Ebola patients escaped from a hospital in Mongbwalu (Ituri Province, DRC) after the facility was attacked by angry relatives. BNO calls it the 3rd such incident in recent days. Direct operational impact: these patients are now in the community and untraced. No primary WHO/DRC MoH confirmation yet; logged for transparency. Worth checking for in next run.
- 2026-05-25 (PM run, xcancel pass): WHO Director-General Tedros, per a French wire (Cerfia citing OMS chief), reportedly said 'l'épidémie nous dépasse' ('the epidemic is overwhelming us') in remarks today, alongside a figure of 'at least 220 suspected Ebola deaths' across DRC and Uganda combined. The 220 figure is materially higher than the 119 suspected deaths in our May 24/25 rows (post-Sunday DRC reclassification) and aligns with the earlier CNN figure of '220 deaths thought to be linked'. Possible interpretations: (a) pre-reclassification gross count, (b) different inclusion criteria, (c) post-Sunday upward trend. No WHO primary source has published the 220 figure with a dated 'as of' attribution. NOT applying as a revision; flagging for next-run reconciliation when WHO Sitrep 02 or DON604 publishes.
- 2026-05-25 (PM run, xcancel pass): India's Directorate General of Civil Aviation (DGCA) issued an SOP for travelers arriving from DRC and Uganda, per @jagritichandra (The Hindu). Includes mandatory passenger declarations and segregated seating for suspected cases on Indian carriers. New cross-border preparedness signal; informational, no impact on case counts.
- 2026-05-25 (PM run, methodology change per Web): Adopted a 'no-dip' rule for cumulative figures. Cumulative metrics (suspected cases, confirmed cases, total cases, suspected deaths) are monotonically non-decreasing. When a source proposes a downward revision, the previous higher value is carried forward UNLESS multiple independent high-value sources confirm the dip. High-value sources: WHO official publications (Sitrep, DON); WHO Director-General statements (briefings, press conferences) even when not yet in a WHO document; DRC MoH official press releases; Uganda MoH official press releases; CDC publications. Single-source dips (e.g., a DRC MoH reclassification carried only by one wire) are held in NOTES pending corroboration. Upward revisions follow normal source-preference rules. Rationale: cumulative drops confuse general readers and almost always reflect definitional changes rather than actual recoveries.
- 2026-05-25 (PM run, applying no-dip rule): Reverted the earlier PM-run revision of May 24 and May 25 suspected-deaths from 204 → 119. The 119 figure (DRC Sunday govt reclassification via Reuters/AP) is a single-source downward proposal not corroborated by other high-value sources. WHO DG Tedros today reportedly cited 'at least 220 suspected Ebola deaths' across DRC + Uganda (per French wire Cerfia citing OMS chief), which contradicts the dip and serves as a high-value upward revision. New values: May 24 suspected deaths 119 → 220 (+85% from PM-run dip, +7.8% from pre-AM-run 204); May 25 suspected deaths 119 → 220 (same). DRC confirmed-cases (101) and confirmed-deaths (10) from the same Sunday update remain in place as upward revisions (no dip issue). The 119 figure is preserved in this NOTES history as the DRC MoH per-source value; if WHO Sitrep 02 or DON604 later confirms the 119 reclassification, the no-dip rule allows applying the dip at that point with multi-source backing.

- 2026-05-25 (schema change, declarations.csv): Added two columns to distinguish CDC's clinician-facing HAN advisory from its traveler-facing Travel Health Notice. New schema: `country, first_report_date, case_status, cdc_advisory, cdc_url, cdc_travel_notice_level, cdc_travel_notice_url, who_status, who_url, notes`. Backfilled both rows: DRC at Level 3 (Reconsider Nonessential Travel, https://wwwnc.cdc.gov/travel/notices/level3/ebola-democratic-republic-of-the-congo), Uganda at Level 1 (Practice Usual Precautions, https://wwwnc.cdc.gov/travel/notices/level1/ebola-uganda). Note: CDC has escalated DRC from Level 2 to Level 3 since the 2026-05-16 CDC Situation Summary publication, which still links to the Level 2 page. METHODOLOGY.md updated to document the new schema and the two-system distinction. Daily-task SKILL.md updated to instruct future runs to check the per-country travel-notice URLs each pass.
- 2026-05-25 (index.html render): Added a "CDC travel notice" column to the public declarations table on index.html, positioned between "CDC advisory" (HAN) and "WHO status" so the two CDC systems read left to right. Cell shows the level + meaning (e.g., "Level 3 (Reconsider Nonessential Travel)") and links to the matching wwwnc.cdc.gov page. Empty `cdc_travel_notice_level` cells fall back to a blank cell. Colspan on the loading and empty-state rows bumped from 6 to 7.

- 2026-05-26 (post-run x.com real-time pass): Web reconnected his Chrome and authorized a live x.com pass. Credentialed-account filter (`from:` query over WHO/WHOAFRO/DrTedros/AfricaCDC/CDCgov/MinSanteRDC/MinofHealthUG/Reuters/AP/AFP/BNODesk/BNOFeed/BNONews/CIDRAP/HelenBranswell/KrutikaKuppalli) surfaced three material findings, no count changes:
  (1) [Reuters @Reuters (~2m at time of pass)](https://x.com/Reuters) reposted the Oxford University ChAdOx1 BDBV vaccine effort: per the [22 May 2026 Oxford University statement](https://www.ox.ac.uk/news/2026-05-22-statement-on-vaccine-efforts-relating-to-the-bundibugyo-ebolavirus-outbreak-in-the) and the [Oxford Vaccine Group page](https://www.ovg.ox.ac.uk/news/statement-on-vaccine-efforts-relating-to-the-bundibugyo-ebolavirus-outbreak-in-the-drc), Oxford's Clinical BioManufacturing Facility is partnering with the Serum Institute of India on a monovalent ChAdOx1-vectored Bundibugyo candidate, with WHO indicating doses could be ready for human clinical trials in 2–3 months. Status: preclinical, NOT approved or in trials yet. story.md content about "no approved vaccine or specific therapeutic" for Bundibugyo remains accurate; Tedros's 25 May briefing already covered the prioritized monoclonal antibodies + obeldesivir, and this Oxford effort is the vaccine track of the same R&D response. No story.md edit on this pass.

This is because one of the initial ebola patients sought treatment from a facility in Kampala. The health workers were among our contacts who were under follow up." This RESOLVES the open question flagged in the 25 May PM xcancel pass (Branswell / Kuppalli speculated about uncertain exposure histories implying undetected community transmission in Kampala). The Uganda MoH confirms the cases reflect known contact-tracing exposure, not undetected community spread. Tightens the narrative; Uganda totals unchanged at 7 conf / 1 death.

- 2026-05-31 (PM2 run, methodology refinement per Web): Added "MoH methodology-change carve-out" to the no-dip rule in METHODOLOGY.md. Rationale: the no-dip rule as originally written required two high-value sources to apply a downward revision. The DRC MoH 30 May ~700-case cleanup (lab capacity ramp-up; suspected cases that subsequently tested negative for Ebola are removed) is a formally-announced definitional reclassification by the official surveillance authority. A definitional cleanup announced by the surveillance source IS the reclassification, by definition; waiting for WHO Sitrep reconciliation in that case is a delay, not a correction. The carve-out waives the two-source threshold when the dip is a MoH (DRC or Uganda) formal announcement and the wire is faithfully carrying it. The standard no-dip rule still applies to wire-only paraphrases of unreconciled recounts. Operationally: exemptions are recorded in `src/validate.py`'s NO_DIP_EXEMPTIONS_TIMESERIES (date → columns) and NO_DIP_EXEMPTIONS_BREAKDOWN ((date, country) → columns) so the no-dip validator skips the named columns on the named row(s). Every exemption gets a NOTES bullet citing the MoH announcement and the figures applied. This was the prototypical case; future MoH cleanups will follow the same pattern.

Real deaths are still accumulating; they're just on the confirmed side now (DRC ran +38 lab confirmations on 31 May for a DRC conf_deaths total of 55). - 2026-05-31 (PM4 run, declarations.csv refresh — surfaced by Web): Audit of declarations.csv against live CDC/WHO sources surfaced several missed events between 2026-05-18 and 2026-05-31. Updates applied: (1) **Uganda CDC Travel Health Notice escalated from Level 1 to Level 2 on 2026-05-27** — explicitly confirmed by the [US Embassy Kampala 28 May health alert](https://ug.usembassy.gov/health-alert-u-s-embassy-kampala-uganda-may-28-2026/) ("On May 27, the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) increased its Travel Health Notice for Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease from a Level 1 to Level 2"). cdc_travel_notice_level changed from "Level 1 (Practice Usual Precautions)" to "Level 2 (Practice Enhanced Precautions)"; cdc_travel_notice_url changed from /level1/ebola-uganda to /level2/ebola-uganda. This was missed in the 2026-05-27 PM run, the 2026-05-28 scheduled run, the 2026-05-30 scheduled run, and the 2026-05-31 AM/PM/PM2/PM3 runs — six consecutive runs that recorded "declarations.csv: no change" without re-verifying the live travel-notice index. Procedural lesson: the daily run's declarations check needs to actually fetch the per-country travel-notice URL and verify the level, not just confirm the URL still resolves. The /level1/ebola-uganda URL still resolves but now serves stale content from a prior SVD outbreak. (2) **DRC CDC Travel Notice remains at Level 3** — re-verified via search-result page title ("Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease in the Democratic Republic of the Congo - Level 3 - Reconsider Nonessential Travel") and the US Embassy Kampala alert linking the /level3/ DRC URL. No change to DRC's cdc_travel_notice_level / URL. (3) **Africa CDC PHECS declaration on 2026-05-18** (Public Health Emergency of Continental Security) — added to both DRC and Uganda notes fields, sourced to the [Africa CDC statement on US travel restrictions](https://africacdc.org/news-item/u-s-travel-restrictions-related-to-the-bundibugyo-ebola-outbreak/). Schema doesn't have an Africa CDC column; the PHECS is documented in notes for now. (4) **US State Department Travel Advisory at Level 4 (Do Not Travel)** for both DRC and Uganda — distinct from the CDC Travel Health Notice (which is at Level 3 / Level 2 respectively); the State Department advisory is a separate US government advisory at the country level. Added to notes for both rows. (5) **US entry restrictions** on non-US passport holders who travelled to DRC, Uganda, or South Sudan in the previous 21 days, in effect from 2026-05-22 and extended to green-card holders the same week — added to notes for both rows. (6) **CDC Title 42 Order** for arrivals from affected countries — added to notes for both rows with the [CDC media release URL](https://www.cdc.gov/media/releases/2026/statement-update-on-title-42-order.html). (7) **US arrivals from affected countries funneled through IAD (from 2026-05-21), ATL (from 2026-05-22), IAH Houston (from 2026-05-26)** for enhanced screening — added to DRC notes. (8) **WHO/DRC Joint Statement of 2026-05-31** linked in DRC notes for the explicit "borders remain open" ask. (9) **Cases now span 13 health zones** (up from 12 noted in original DRC entry) — updated DRC notes. (10) **Sud-Kivu first lab-confirmed case 2026-05-25** — added to DRC notes (this was logged in the timeseries.csv May 25 primary_source but not surfaced in declarations.csv). Validator passes; no schema change. Note: declarations.csv's current schema does not have columns for US State Department Travel Advisory or Africa CDC PHECS. If these become recurring fields worth tracking with structured cells, the schema should be expanded; for now the notes field carries them.

- 2026-05-31 (PM5 run, METHODOLOGY.md country-add rule + index.html confirmed-deaths column — surfaced by Web): Two structural updates. (a) Added "Adding a country to declarations.csv" section to METHODOLOGY.md with explicit criteria for when a new country row gets added: own confirmed/suspected cases, OR own country-specific CDC Travel Health Notice, OR own country-specific CDC HAN entry (or multi-country HAN that names the country), OR own WHO country-specific declaration, OR non-US national health authority outbreak-specific advisory with stable primary-source URL. Explicitly clarified that a country appearing in another country's advisory (e.g., South Sudan in the US entry restrictions) does NOT warrant its own row; cross-country effects are captured in the affected countries' notes fields. Also documented the verification protocol on each daily run: a "declarations.csv: no change" report must mean the agent actually fetched the per-country CDC Travel Notice URL and verified the level on the page, plus scanned the CDC Travel Notices index. URL-resolves != level-unchanged (the Uganda /level1/ URL kept serving stale Sudan-virus content after CDC moved BVD to /level2/). Audit of currently-named-in-other-countries' advisories: South Sudan (in US entry restrictions and US State Dept Do Not Travel) does not have its own CDC HAN, CDC Travel Health Notice, or WHO country-specific advisory for this outbreak as of 2026-05-31; no row added. Other countries imposing their own measures (Canada, Bahamas) have their own national-level travel advisories but those are not in scope under the current schema (CDC/WHO only); if Web wants to track non-US national advisories, schema extension is warranted. WHO's 210/17 figures are HIGHER than WHO DON605's 125/17 (29 May cutoff) but LOWER than DRC MoH's 282/42 (31 May situation report) and BNO's June 1 graphic (352 confirmed globally including 343 DRC + 9 Uganda; 49 confirmed deaths including 48 DRC + 1 Uganda). WHO is partially reconciling the DRC MoH lab catch-up but hasn't caught up fully. Per source-preference order (WHO > DRC MoH > BNO), if WHO had a publication-grade 210 figure, we'd use it; but the 210 is reported via UN News with no clean WHO publication URL (DON606 not yet published; Sitrep 03 expected 2 Jun). For this run we use the BNO @BNOFeed June 1 graphic as the primary source for the June 1 row (BNO graphic explicitly attributes to MoH DR Congo + MoH Uganda + WHO and is dated June 1 — fresher than the WHO Sunday update). WHO Sitrep 03 will reconcile.
- 2026-06-01 (scheduled run, narrative milestones from UN News June 1): Five recoveries to date in DRC — four nurses discharged from hospital + one lab worker cleared the prior Thursday. 16 health workers have contracted Ebola in DRC during this outbreak. WHO has handed over a 24-bed refurbished Ebola Treatment Centre in Bunia to DRC health authorities, total capacity 60 beds; WHO is also setting up an annexe with up to 42 beds. Tedros (Bunia): "Ebola caused by the Bundibugyo virus can be survived with good medical care, and some people here in Ituri have already recovered. Seeking care early makes a real difference…It is not without hope." Three candidate therapeutics for treatment have been prioritized for clinical trials: monoclonal antibodies MBP 134 and maftivimab, and the antiviral remdesivir. For prevention, the oral antiviral obeldesivir is being prioritized as a post-exposure measure. Two candidate vaccines have been identified for evaluation once doses become available. CFR estimated 30–50% for Bundibugyo strain.

- 2026-06-06 (scheduled run, narrative milestones): (1) CDC released three MMWRs on 5 June: a Notes from the Field report calling this the largest Bundibugyo outbreak on record (first detected among healthcare-worker clusters), a dedicated US risk assessment (risk to the general US public low; importation likelihood low; sustained US secondary transmission low), and a Center for Forecasting and Outbreak Analytics modeling report. (2) The CFA model (a planning tool, not a forecast) warns the outbreak could rival or exceed the 2014-2016 West Africa epidemic without strong intervention: >20,000 cases in 2 of 3 simulations over 3 months at 20% case isolation within 2 days of symptom onset; 94% probability of staying under 10,000 cases at 70% isolation. Scenarios bracketed 50/100/200 deaths as of 24 May against 20/50/70/95% isolation. (3) Dr. Satish Pillai (CDC Ebola incident manager) said the ground situation is at the lower end of the isolation scenarios. (4) No evidence of community transmission in Kampala — all Uganda cases trace to DRC travel or to those travelers. (5) WHO and Africa CDC launched a ~$518M joint response plan; US has withdrawn from WHO (CDC declined to confirm whether US funds will flow into the WHO-led plan). (6) DRC contact tracing up from ~9% to 55% (target 90%); 4,000+ Africa CDC test kits delivered; results within 24h. (7) DRC Health Minister called US travel restrictions "discriminatory" ([Washington Times, 5 June](https://www.washingtontimes.com/news/2026/jun/5/congo-health-minister-calls-us-ebola-travel-restrictions/)). (8) US doctor infected in DRC fully recovered in Berlin (Today's News / wire).

- 2026-06-16 (scheduled run, suspected/probable taxonomy decision — FLAG for Web): WHO Sitreps report only "confirmed" and "probable", not "suspected". suspected_global is held at 116 (carried from the 5 June WHO DON cleaned baseline) because Sitrep 05 provides no suspected figure to reconcile against; this is consistent with the prior Sitrep 04 handling (June 7 row also held suspected at 116). The single Uganda probable case and probable death reported by Sitrep 05 were NOT folded into the global suspected_global / suspected_deaths_global columns: doing so would resurrect the deliberately-zeroed suspected-deaths column (0->1) for one marginal probable death from a single source, and "probable" is a distinct WHO category from the tracker's "suspected". The probable case/death is recorded in the June 14 country_breakdown Uganda note and in the timeseries source cell instead. Net effect: the tracker's total_global (943, = 116 suspected + 827 confirmed) runs above WHO's confirmed+probable total (828) by the held 115 suspected, which is the no-dip rule biasing toward the higher figure as designed. [DON612](https://www.who.int/emergencies/disease-outbreak-news/item/2026-DON612), published 3 July 2026 (data as of 1 July DRC / 2 July Uganda), is the first reconciled WHO product since DON608 (19 June) and supersedes the BNO running count as source #2. DON612 figures: DR Congo 1,460 confirmed / 452 confirmed deaths (crude CFR 30.9%), 213 recovered, 36 affected health zones (24/36 Ituri, 11/35 North Kivu, 1/34 South Kivu; 21 active in past 21 days), 102 HCW cases / 25 HCW deaths; Uganda 20 confirmed / 2 deaths (imported) + 1 probable case who died, 16 recovered, no new case since 21 June, affected-HCW count revised 5->4; France 1 confirmed / 0 deaths (24 June imported case); global TOTAL 1,481 confirmed / 454 confirmed deaths / 229 recovered. LOOKBACK REVISION applied to the 2026-07-02 row (source-preference: DON #2 outranks BNO; within 3-day window): confirmed_deaths_global 449->454 (+5, +1.1%); confirmed 1481 unchanged (matches DON612 exactly); suspected 116 / suspected_deaths 0 carried; total_global 1597 unchanged. country_breakdown 2026-07-02 DRC deaths 447->452 (+5); DRC confirmed 1460 unchanged. - 2026-07-13 (run, MATERIAL NARRATIVE - outbreak geography): the DRC INSP formally designated Haut-Uele and Tshopo as epidemic zones, taking the outbreak from 3 affected provinces to 5. Tshopo has 4 cases including 2 deaths; Haut-Uele has 1 death. INSP judges all of them imported from Nia Nia health zone in Ituri, but says it is "necessary and appropriate to consider these two provinces as an epidemic zone". Tshopo's capital is Kisangani, one of the DRC's largest cities; Haut-Uele borders South Sudan and the Central African Republic ([Reuters via CNBC Africa](https://www.cnbcafrica.com/2026/congo-says-ebola-outbreak-spreads-to-provinces-haut-uele-and-tshopo)). WHO DON612 (3 July) had already counted the Wamba (Haut-Uele) and Kisangani (Tshopo) cases under Nia Nia; the change here is the DRC's own designation, not a new case count. story.md "Latest update" refreshed; declarations.csv DRC notes field extended to record the 5-province scope. No timeseries figures changed by this designation.

- 2026-07-23 (Web-directed change): added a fourth chart, week-over-week percent change in active cases. src/generate_charts.py gains compute_active_wow + render_active_wow_chart: for each date, mean active over the trailing 7 days (t-6..t) vs. the prior 7 days (t-13..t-7), plotted as (current_mean/prior_mean - 1)*100. The two adjacent 7-day averaging windows smooth daily spikes so the line reads as a growth-rate trend (above zero = active pool still growing). Undefined until 14 days of active history; skipped where the prior-window mean is non-positive. Outputs YYYY-MM-DD_ebola-active-wow.png + latest-active-wow.png; wired into main() as the fourth published chart and added to index.html. build_xlsx.py Charts sheet gains an "Active w/w % (7-day avg)" column. METHODOLOGY.md Charts section and README updated (four charts now). As of 2026-07-22 the w/w change reads about +7.5%, down from ~+200% in late May: the active caseload is still growing but the growth rate has decelerated sharply.

Y gridlines fixed at 0/10/20/30/50/100/200 percent (mirrored negative) per Web; minor ticks off. The zero-crossing fill shading was dropped for a cleaner read. - 2026-08-03 (run): SELF-CORRECTION made before commit. The first draft of this run's 2026-08-02 primary_source cell and story.md entry both claimed the +42 recovered figure was "the largest single-day recovery figure of the outbreak" and that recoveries exceeding deaths was happening "for the first time." Both claims were checked against the series before the commit and both were FALSE. Recovered daily deltas were computed across the whole series: the largest is +101 on 2026-07-19 (a catch-up after the 15-18 July BNO publication gap, not a true single day), which makes +42 the second-largest. Days on which the recovered delta exceeded the confirmed-deaths delta number 10 including 2026-08-02, running back to 2026-06-05, most recently 2026-07-13. The defensible fact for this date is different and narrower: derived active cases FELL, 1,387 -> 1,383, the 10th falling-active day of the outbreak and the first since 2026-07-13, because the day's outflow (42 + 36 = 78) exceeded the inflow (+74). A fall of 4 on a base of 1,383 is inside the noise of a running wire count and is explicitly NOT read as a turn in either the CSV cell or story.md. Both texts were rewritten to the checked version. Logged because the failure mode (asserting a superlative from a single day's graphic without querying the series) is the kind of thing that would otherwise propagate into the public narrative.
- 2026-08-03 (run): TOOLING NOTE, caught by Gate B before commit. data/timeseries.csv is stored with CRLF line endings. The in-place text patch used this run to correct the 2026-08-02 primary_source cell was written through Python text mode, which normalised the whole file to LF and produced a 79-insertion / 78-deletion whole-file diff on what was a one-cell edit. Gate B's 100-line ceiling caught it. Line endings were restored to CRLF byte-for-byte and the staged diff dropped to 1 insertion on timeseries.csv, 24 insertions / 6 deletions across the run. No data was altered by either step; validate.py passes before and after. For future runs: patch these CSVs in BINARY mode, or re-normalise to CRLF after any text-mode write.

- 2026-08-05 (run): SEATING ADJUDICATION for the 3 August row. The AP copy says the 3,802 / 1,707 figure is "as of Tuesday", i.e. 4 August, the same day AP published. That cannot be right. The decisive check is the MoH's own statement in the 3 August bulletin that 22 new recoveries were recorded in the last 24 hours: 727 + 22 = 749 exactly, which pins 727 to 2 August and 749 to 3 August. Seated accordingly. The wire's "as of" wording was treated as unreliable and the arithmetic as authoritative; flagged here rather than silently resolved.

- 2026-08-09 (run): MATERIAL, no effect on any count. On Friday 7 August the WHO Technical Advisory Group on Candidate Vaccine Prioritization recommended that Ervebo, the only licensed Ebola vaccine, be prioritised for inclusion in a randomised clinical trial in the context of this outbreak. Ervebo is licensed against Zaire ebolavirus; there is no licensed vaccine for Bundibugyo. The recommendation rests on preliminary animal data suggesting partial cross-protection: three of four vaccinated non-human primates survived Bundibugyo challenge against one of four controls, and an unpublished ferret study found 100% protection from a research-grade formulation. TAG-CVP explicitly cautions that a Bundibugyo-specific vaccine remains the preferred option and that "expectations need to be managed about its likely protective effect... as efficacy against symptomatic disease and transmission may not be high, whereas protection against a fatal outcome may be achieved". Gavi holds a 500,000-dose Ervebo stockpile and will release doses for the trials, some already in DRC. Two Bundibugyo-specific candidates are in early human trials and a third is being readied. This is a distinct development from the Oxford ChAdOx1 BDBV trial first dosed 24 July, which is logged on the 25 July row.

Uganda is still named in the Level 2 notice title and key points twelve days after its 28 July end-of-outbreak declaration, so CDC has still not reflected it. HAN-00530 and the 17 May PHEIC unchanged. The US boarding restriction language is unchanged on both pages.
- 2026-08-09 (run): REAL-TIME PASS COMPLETE but partially degraded. Claude in Chrome dropped mid-run twice (`list_connected_browsers` returned an empty array once, and one `computer` call returned "not connected") and recovered both times; the BNO check was completed in full. A tooling lesson worth recording: `get_page_text` on the @BNOFeed timeline returned a STALE first article, showing the AUGUST 6 graphic's figures as the newest post when a screenshot of the same tab showed a newer AUGUST 7 post above it. Had this run trusted `get_page_text` alone it would have missed a day. On x.com, screenshot the timeline rather than relying on `get_page_text`, which appears to return only the first `<article>` and can serve a cached one. The credentialed x.com search pass (`ebola outbreak`, Latest) was run and returned only anonymous and conspiracy accounts in the live window, all filtered out under the METHODOLOGY rules; nothing superseding, nothing to follow to an institutional source.

- 2026-08-10 (run): FIRST FALL IN CFR IN FIVE DAYS, and it is one day, not a turn. The 8 August row carries the smallest one-day confirmed-death increment since 20 July (+29, against +37, +49, +50 and +44 on the four preceding days) alongside the largest case increment since 5 August (+89). DRC crude CFR falls 45.8% -> 45.5%, global 45.6% -> 45.3%, after five consecutive days of outbreak highs. Contact tracing climbs to 83.4% from 77.7% on the previous bulletin and 75% at the WHO/Africa CDC joint mission of 4-5 August, the first time it has come within two points of the 85% operational threshold. Read against the derived active (live) case line, which still RISES 1,423 -> 1,465, this is one day of a running count and is logged as such. The 2 August falling-active day is the precedent for not over-reading a single day.
- 2026-08-10 (run): SOURCE-INTERNAL ANOMALY, not folded in and not reconciled. The Ministry bulletin reports 595 patients in isolation or hospitalised, against 721 on the bulletin labelled 6 August and 694 on the one labelled 5 August, a fall of 126 on a day when derived active cases rose by 42. Isolation occupancy is a point-in-time bed count on a different basis from the cumulative surveillance columns, so it does not contradict any figure in the CSVs, but a 17% one-day drop in occupancy while the active pool grows is worth watching. Flagged in case a later WHO Sitrep explains it (discharge batch, reporting change, or beds lost).
- 2026-08-10 (run): MATERIAL, no effect on any count. Africa CDC Director-General Jean Kaseya told a media briefing on Thursday 6 August that he and WHO Director-General Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus had agreed on 5 August to commission a study into whether the Bundibugyo virus is mutating, "because the level of severity of this Bundibugyo outbreak is unprecedented" ([Health Policy Watch, 7 August](https://healthpolicy-watch.news/alarming-ebola-spread-raises-question-of-viral-mutation/); carried by The Guardian the same week). This is a stated intention to investigate, not a finding; no sequencing result has been published and nothing here changes a column. From the same briefing and not folded in: 67% of Ebola deaths last week occurred in communities rather than health facilities, usually identified by post-mortem testing; around 140 health workers have been infected; DRC averages about 10 contacts recorded per patient against 40+ in Uganda; Ebola treatment centres in Nord-Kivu ran at 128-157% occupancy against 56-69% in Ituri; and Kaseya attributed Uganda's ~10% case fatality against DRC's ~44% partly to every Uganda patient receiving remdesivir, with a remdesivir and MPP134 trial under way in DRC that has recruited only 68 people so far. Separately, on 6 August DRC authorities intercepted a passenger boat carrying about 200 people roughly 65 km from Kinshasa after a passenger with suspected Ebola symptoms disembarked and died ([Reuters](https://www.reuters.com/business/healthcare-pharmaceuticals/congo-intercepts-passenger-boat-near-kinshasa-that-carried-suspected-ebola-case-2026-08-06/)); all passengers were tested and the outbreak has not reached Kinshasa, population roughly 17 million.

- 2026-08-11 (run): EIGHTH CONFIRMATION OF THE 1-DAY LEAD, still not applied, plus a publication-lag wrinkle worth recording. Both wires state the data date independently of each other and of the Ministry: EFE says the bulletin carries "datos recopilados hasta el 8 de agosto" and Europa Press describes it as the evening bulletin of the Institut National de Sante Publique "que abarca hasta el pasado sabado", Saturday 8 August. The row therefore seats on 9 August under the standing as-of-plus-one convention. THE WRINKLE: the Ministry published nothing at all on 9 August, so this bulletin landed two calendar days after its data date rather than the usual one. The convention is applied to the DATA date, not the posting date, so the lag does not move the seating; it only means the tracker had to wait an extra day for the figure. ECDC describes the change from the previous report as "89 new confirmed cases and 21 deaths", but its own cumulative figures move 1,887 to 1,916, a rise of 29, which is what this tracker carries and what the Ministry bulletins give. - 2026-08-11 (run): THE 8 AUGUST DIP DID NOT HOLD, which is the point of not over-reading a single day. The 8 August row carried the first CFR fall in five days on a +29 death increment. The 9 August row puts the increment back at +44 and DRC crude CFR resumes climbing 45.5% to 45.6%, global 45.3% to 45.5%. Derived active (live) cases also keep rising, 1,465 to 1,485. Two things did carry through. Contact tracing went 83.4% to 84.6%, its highest of the outbreak and within half a point of the 85% operational threshold, so the one indicator upstream of everything else has now improved on three consecutive bulletins from 75% at the WHO/Africa CDC mission of 4-5 August. And the isolation occupancy anomaly flagged on the 8 August row has largely reversed: 595 patients in isolation went back to 722, against 721 on the bulletin labelled 6 August. That reads as a one-bulletin reporting artefact rather than a loss of beds or a discharge batch. Kept in notes rather than reconciled into any column; occupancy is a point-in-time bed count on a different basis from the cumulative columns.

Rows added for 10 and 11 August, so the series is contiguous from 28 July through 11 August. 10 August carries the DRC MoH/INSP bulletin as of 9 August (DR Congo 4,381 confirmed / 869 recovered / 2,011 deaths), 11 August carries the bulletin as of 10 August (DR Congo 4,449 / 886 / 2,061). 27 July REMAINS the only open gap in the series (unchanged call: its only candidate source, the BNO JULY 27 graphic, re-publishes the Sitrep 11 as-of-26-July figure verbatim and would create a false-flat 26==27 duplicate).
- 2026-08-12 (run): SEATING CALL on the as-of-9-August bulletin, recorded because it was an inference rather than a read label. No source this run stated the bulletin's own date. AFP and AP both give 4,381 / 2,011 "as of Tuesday", which is the publication day, not the data day; UN News (French) dates the publication to "la nuit de lundi a mardi" and names the previous bilan as "date du 8 aout"; Radio Okapi independently confirms the previous one was "publie le 10 aout et arrete a la date du 8 aout 2026". The data date was settled on arithmetic: a cumulative surveillance count cannot skip a day, and +87 confirmed is a one-day step at the current rate against the ~170 that two days would imply, so this is the as-of-9-August bulletin. The separately-published as-of-10-August bulletin (4,449 / 2,061) then seats cleanly on 11 August, which closes both gaps with no overlap and no false flat. If a later WHO or ECDC product dates these figures differently, the rows come back under the normal lookback path.
- 2026-08-12 (run): RECOVERED for the 10 August row is RECONSTRUCTED, not sourced. No wire carried a recovered figure for the as-of-9-August bulletin. The BNO @BNOFeed AUGUST 11 graphic reports DR Congo 886 recovered (+17), so the prior-day figure is 886 - 17 = 869, and the same graphic's confirmed and deaths deltas (4,449 - 68 = 4,381; 2,061 - 50 = 2,011) reproduce the two wire-sourced fields exactly, which is what justifies trusting the third. This is the same reconstruction method used for the 2026-06-03 recovered figure and documented in METHODOLOGY "Recovered and active cases". Flagged here so a later WHO reconciliation of recovered is applied to this date first.
- 2026-08-12 (run): MILESTONE. DRC confirmed deaths crossed 2,000 on the 10 August row (2,011) and global confirmed deaths crossed 2,000 the same day (2,013), 88 days after the first case. Julien Harneis, UN Senior Ebola Coordinator, quoted by UN News: the outbreak "has passed 2,000 deaths, half of them in the last 20 days". The first thousand deaths took about nine weeks, the second about three. On the 11 August row DRC crude CFR crosses 46% for the first time (46.3%) and global CFR reaches 46.2%; both are outbreak highs.
- 2026-08-12 (run): FIRST FLAT ACTIVE DAY SINCE 2 AUGUST. Derived active (live) cases go 1,485 -> 1,501 -> 1,502. On 11 August the day's outflow (17 recovered + 50 deaths = 67) almost exactly matches the inflow (+68 confirmed). Logged, not read as a turn: the flattening comes from the inflow slowing to meet an outflow still dominated by deaths rather than recoveries, and one day inside a running bulletin count is noise. The 2 August precedent (a fall of 4 that did not hold) is the reason for the caution.
- 2026-08-12 (run): CONTACT TRACING REVERSES. 83.4% (as of 7 Aug) -> 84.6% (as of 8 Aug) -> 82.7% (as of 10 Aug), so the three-bulletin climb toward the 85% operational threshold has broken. Isolation occupancy 595 -> 722 -> 716. No figure exists for the as-of-9-August bulletin on either indicator. Neither is a tracker column; recorded because contact-tracing coverage is the leading indicator the WHO/Africa CDC mission singled out on 4-5 August.

- 2026-08-12 (Web-directed change): the 23 July spike is now ANNOTATED, not redistributed. Web asked whether the mid-July catch-up could be spread back across the history, on the concern that it clouds the week-over-week calculation. Investigated and declined the CSV edit, with his agreement to handle it in the chart layer instead. The decisive evidence: WHO Weekly Sitrep 10 PUBLISHED ON 28 JULY, five days after the 23 July INSP provincial data review, and still reported DR Congo 2,423 confirmed as of 19 July; the entire increment lands in the 20-26 July week of Sitrep 11. WHO had the reconciled data in hand and did not push it back into pre-19-July history, so it books these on report date exactly as this tracker does. Redistributing would have broken the exact-match property against Sitreps 10, 12 and 13 on every date before the event, and no source publishes the onset window the redistribution would need (Bloomberg names the databases merged, not the dates the cases fall on). New METHODOLOGY.md section "Surveillance-reconciliation events" records the reasoning.
- 2026-08-12 (Web-directed change): src/generate_charts.py gains RECONCILIATION_EVENTS (date -> label; currently just 2026-07-23 "INSP provincial data reconciliation") and mark_reconciliation_events, a dotted vertical marker drawn on ALL FOUR charts, deliberately matching the existing mark_baseline_resets furniture because both flags say the same thing: this bar is an artifact of how the data was reported, not a day of transmission. The label carries a semi-opaque white bbox because, unlike a reset, a reconciliation lands mid-chart on the tallest bar in the series.
- 2026-08-12 (Web-directed change): the week-over-week chart gains a shaded band over the distorted stretch and a dashed reconciliation-adjusted line (compute_reconciliation_adjusted_active). Method: estimate a normal day as the MEDIAN of daily deltas over RECONCILIATION_BASELINE_SPAN=5 days either side, excluding the event, baseline-reset days, other reconciliation dates and zero-delta carry-forward days (27 July); treat the remainder as backlog (290 cases, 199 deaths against a median normal day of 79 cases / 37 deaths); raise the CUMULATIVE across the RECONCILIATION_BACKLOG_DAYS=21 window ending on the event. The event date is left untouched so the two series rejoin there and before the window opens, bounding the counterfactual at both ends. Measured effect: divergence runs 3 July to 4 August and is exactly zero from 5 August on; the late-July w/w peak falls from 25.1% (1 Aug) to 19.9%, mid-July rises (20 July 7.2% -> 10.2%), and the current 11 August reading is UNCHANGED at 6.8%. So the artifact was never touching the latest number, only a two-week stretch of history.
- 2026-08-12 (Web-directed change): TWO ASSUMPTIONS recorded explicitly because neither is published anywhere and both are visible on the chart caption. (1) The 21-day backlog window, chosen as roughly the interval since the prior reconciliation; a round number, not a finding. (2) Even allocation across it; real under-reporting is lumpier. Both constants are deliberately easy to change so the sensitivity can be re-run. The reported line stays the headline series; the adjusted line is dashed, unmarked and labelled an estimate so it cannot be read as an equal-status measurement.
- 2026-08-12 (Web-directed change): TWO IMPLEMENTATION BUGS caught during the build and recorded so the reasoning is not lost. (a) The first version spread the backlog PROPORTIONALLY across the entire prior series. Rejected on sight of the rendered chart: the shaded divergence band covered nearly the whole timeline, because perturbing every prior date changes the May and June week-over-week ratios too, where the Ituri/Nord-Kivu database gap has no bearing. Replaced with the bounded 21-day window. (b) The rewrite initially re-accumulated cumulative FROM the daily deltas, which is wrong in this codebase: load_data deliberately forces the daily delta to zero on BASELINE_RESET_DATES while the cumulative steps, so deltas do not sum back to cumulative and the whole May-June history was corrupted. Fixed by adjusting the cumulative series directly. A third error (adding the backlog with the wrong sign, which lowered rather than raised the pre-event cumulative) was caught by a sanity assertion that the adjusted series must equal the reported series on the event date and outside the window. That assertion is the reason to keep this computation testable.

- 2026-08-12 (second run of the day, evening): story.md "Latest update" REPLACED (single-entry rule) to lead on a material narrative development the morning run predates. WHO Director-General Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus told reporters on Wednesday 12 August that the outbreak is on track to eclipse the 2014-16 West Africa epidemic (>11,000 deaths of >28,000 cases) as the deadliest on record: "The outbreak had a big head start, still way ahead of us, and we're playing catch-up." WHO's Abdirahman Mahamud put the expected peak at six months as the moderate scenario, with a longer scenario of nine to twelve months ([AP via ABC News, 12 August](https://abcnews.com/Health/wireStory/congos-ebola-outbreak-track-surpass-deadliest-history-135580129); carried the same day by Washington Post, US News, Al Jazeera). EDITORIAL CALL: the entry states the arithmetic alongside the projection, because "on track to surpass the deadliest" is a statement about PACE, not position. At 4,470 confirmed and 2,063 deaths the outbreak is roughly a sixth of West Africa's case count and a fifth of its deaths; West Africa took about two years. Without that framing a reader can take the headline as a statement of current scale. The Sitrep 13 substance from the morning entry is retained in condensed form so the replaced entry still stands alone. No figures in any CSV are affected.

Per-date sources: 14 August from the DRC Ministry of Health situation report published Friday 14 August as reported by [Xinhua, filed Kinshasa 14 August](https://french.news.cn/20260815/a00c1c2dfeda46a68fc7b502d182a8e9/c.html) (DR Congo 4,727 confirmed / 2,214 deaths / 976 recovered); 15 August from the [BNO News @BNOFeed Daily Ebola Update posted 15 August](https://x.com/BNOFeed) (global Cases 4,864 +116 / Recovered 1,025 +30 / Deaths 2,274 +58); 16 August from [BNO News, "DR Congo Ebola outbreak becomes 2nd deadliest on record", 16 August](https://bnonews.com/index.php/2026/08/dr-congo-ebola-outbreak-becomes-2nd-deadliest-on-record/) (DR Congo 4,945 / 2,325 / 1,040, global 4,966 / 2,327). 17 AUGUST LEFT AS A GAP: no source has published an as-of-17-August figure at 00:55 on the 17th. To backfill next run.
- 2026-08-17 (run): THREE-WAY ARITHMETIC RECONCILIATION, worth recording because it is the cleanest cross-source check the tracker has had. The 14 August Ministry figure (DRC 4,727 / 2,214 / 976) was published by Xinhua only as a cumulative snapshot with no delta. BNO's 15 August post states its own deltas (+116 / +58 / +30) against a prior reading; subtracting those from the 15 August globals gives 4,748 / 2,216 / 995, which decomposes exactly to Ministry DRC 4,727 / 2,214 / 976 plus final Uganda 20 / 2 / 18 plus France 1 / 0 / 1. The BNO 16 August article's stated deltas (+102 / +53 / +34) then work backwards from DRC 4,945 / 2,325 / 1,040 to DRC 4,843 / 2,272 / 1,006 on 15 August, which is exactly the BNO 15 August global minus Uganda and France. Ministry, BNO wire post and BNO article agree on every cell across three dates with no rounding slack. The 15 August per-country split is therefore derived arithmetic rather than a read figure, and is labelled as such in country_breakdown.csv.
- 2026-08-17 (run): SUSPECTED COLUMN HELD AT 116, NOT MOVED TO 306. BNO's 16 August article reports "another 306 suspected cases are under investigation" in DRC. This is a point-in-time pending-laboratory count; suspected_global on this tracker is a CUMULATIVE column, last set by the 5 June WHO DON cleaned baseline at 116 and flat since. The two measures have different denominators, and writing 306 into the cumulative column would produce a 190-case single-day step that no source has published as cumulative. BASIS-MISMATCH HOLD, not a no-dip hold (the proposed move is upward). Held pending a WHO Sitrep or DON that states cumulative suspected explicitly. Flagged because BNO has not previously carried a suspected figure at all, and if it starts carrying one daily the tracker will need an explicit decision from Web on whether to open a separate active-suspected column rather than contaminate the cumulative one.

This makes the outbreak the deadliest in DRC history on deaths as well as on cases, and the second deadliest on record globally after West Africa 2014-16 (11,310 deaths). Global cumulative total also crossed 5,000 on the 16 August row at 5,082.

- 2026-08-17 (second run of the day, evening): RECOVERED BASIS DISCREPANCY OPENED, +2 HELD, FLAGGED FOR WEB. DON615 states "at least 986 patients have recovered including 965 in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, 18 from Uganda, two in Germany and one from France" as of 12 August. The tracker's 13 August row carries recovered_global 984 = DRC 965 + Uganda 18 + France 1. The gap is exactly the two patients who were diagnosed in DRC, medically evacuated to Germany (a US citizen in May and a second in July, both confirmed by ECDC and CDC), and recovered there. DON615 counts those two inside DRC's 4665 confirmed but OUTSIDE DRC's 965 recovered, so WHO's recovered total spans an entity the tracker does not carry. HELD at 984 rather than applied, on the same basis-mismatch reasoning as this morning's suspected-column hold: recovered_global on this tracker is defined as the sum of its tracked-country rows, and writing 986 into it without a Germany row would make the global stop decomposing into country_breakdown.csv and would break the validator's country-sum cross-check the first day it lands on the latest row. FLAG FOR WEB: the tracker's recovered column therefore runs 2 low against WHO from the July medevac onward, and will keep doing so. Two ways to close it, both needing your call - add Germany as a recovery-only entity in country_breakdown.csv and declarations.csv (consistent with how France was added on 24 June), or leave the 2 out and state the exclusion in METHODOLOGY's "Recovered and active cases" section. Not a no-dip case; nothing was revised.

- 2026-08-18 (run): CSV QUOTING DEFECT FOUND AND REPAIRED ON THE 2026-08-04 ROW. FORMATTING ONLY, NO FIGURE CHANGED. The row's primary_source field contained nine bare `"` characters that were never doubled to `""` (they start at the BNO graphic citation `("81 DAYS SINCE FIRST CASE")` and run through the AP and Anadolu quotes to the end of the field). A conformant CSV reader terminates the quoted field at the first bare quote, so `csv.DictReader` was returning that one row with eleven extra unnamed columns and a truncated primary_source. The numeric columns sit before primary_source, so validate.py, generate_charts.py and build_xlsx.py were all reading the right numbers and nothing downstream was wrong; the damage was confined to anyone parsing the published file for the citation text. Repaired by unescaping the field, re-escaping every quote, and rewriting the single line. Verified after: all 93 pre-existing rows parse to exactly 9 fields, and the 08-04 numerics are byte-identical (116 / 3895 / 4011 / 0 / 1753 / 768, timestamp 2026-08-05). This is a file-format correction, not a lookback revision, and it is the only row in either CSV with the defect (country_breakdown.csv scanned clean). SURFACED FOR WEB in case he would rather the historical bytes had been left alone.

This run committed with explicit paths rather than `git add -A` so they could not be swept into the commit. They are NOT deleted and NOT gitignored, because deleting is irreversible and `.gitignore` sits outside the commit-gate allowlist. SURFACED FOR WEB: adding `.fuse_hidden*` to `.gitignore` would make future `git add -A` runs safe again.

- 2026-08-19 (run): LARGEST SINGLE-DAY INCREMENTS OF THE SERIES TO DATE, FLAGGED NOT SMOOTHED. +187 confirmed and +98 deaths on 18 August against a trailing two-week range of +62 to +117 confirmed and +29 to +67 deaths. Deaths roughly doubled the recent daily run-rate. This is a same-day BNO running count with no WHO reconciliation behind it, and BNO running counts have historically overshot later WHO reconciliation by ~15-18%. A jump of this shape has twice before turned out to be a database catch-up rather than a day of transmission (see "Surveillance-reconciliation events", 23 July). NO redistribution, NO smoothing, NO annotation added yet: the tracker is by report date and the increment stays on the day it was reported. If WHO Sitrep 15 (expected ~25 August, data as of ~23 August) shows the increment was a reconciliation, a RECONCILIATION_EVENTS entry for 2026-08-18 will be added then, with the Sitrep as the citation.

- 2026-08-19 (run): WRITE METHOD CHANGED MID-RUN AFTER A ROUND-TRIP DEFECT WAS CAUGHT, NO DATA CHANGE, SURFACED FOR WEB. The first attempt appended this run's rows by reading country_breakdown.csv with Python's csv reader and rewriting the whole file. A pre-commit byte-diff caught that this silently rewrote ONE historical record - the 2026-08-04 DRC row - whose primary_source field contains an UNESCAPED inner double-quote pair around the phrase 'as of Monday'. The file as committed is therefore not strictly-valid CSV at that point; Python's reader accepts it in non-strict mode, but writing the parsed value back re-escaped the quotes and moved them (the opening quote was dropped and two others were doubled). No numeric field and no URL was affected, and the parsed value is unchanged, but the stored source text of a historical row was being altered outside the lookback policy. FIX APPLIED: country_breakdown.csv was rebuilt as the original bytes from HEAD plus the three new 18 August rows appended, so the staged diff is now 3 insertions and 0 deletions and every prior byte is untouched. timeseries.csv was checked the same way and was already a clean pure append. STANDING NOTE FOR FUTURE RUNS: append to these CSVs by writing new rows onto the existing bytes, not by round-tripping the whole file through a csv reader/writer. The 2026-08-04 unescaped-quote defect is still in the file and is left alone; fixing it is a deliberate edit to a historical row and is Web's call.
- 2026-08-21 (run): PRIMARY SOURCE UPGRADE. The DRC INSP publishes a dated daily SitRep PDF for this epidemic at [insp.cd/ebola-17eme-epidemie](https://insp.cd/ebola-17eme-epidemie/), and each PDF prints BOTH a "Date de rapportage" and a "Date de publication" on its front page. Prior runs read the INSP listing only for the sitrep NUMBER and took figures from BNO or from wires quoting the sitrep. This run read the PDFs directly. They carry cumulative confirmed cases, confirmed deaths, cumulative gueris, patients in isolation, the per-province and per-health-zone split, and the stated 24h deltas, which makes them a strictly better source than any wire on every column this tracker holds, and they arrive a day earlier than WHO reconciliation. PDF URLs follow the pattern https://insp.cd/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/NN.pdf where NN is the sitrep number, so freshness can be checked by fetching NN+1 directly. STANDING RECOMMENDATION FOR WEB: promote the INSP daily SitRep to the source preference order in METHODOLOGY, between WHO DON and CDC, or arguably above WHO DON for DRC-only columns given the one-day lead. Not applied this run because editing the preference order is a methodology change and is your call.
- 2026-08-21 (run): SEATING RULE RESOLVED AND ONE ROW RE-SEATED. The tracker's Report Date is the INSP PUBLICATION date, not the INSP reporting date. Confirmed against three consecutive rows that were already correct: SitRep 093 (rapportage 15/08, publication 16/08) is the 16 August row at DRC 4,945 / 2,325 / 1,040; SitRep 094 (rapportage 16/08, publication 17/08) is the 17 August row at 5,021 / 2,378 / 1,061, which is also what WHO Sitrep 14 "data as of 16 August" gave. So the "WHO as of D lands on row D+1" convention logged on 18 August is the same rule stated in WHO's vocabulary: WHO's "as of D" is INSP's rapportage D, published D+1. BNO's graphic labels the INSP REPORTING date, which is one day behind the publication date, and that is where the error entered. On 19 August the run seated BNO's post-gap catch-up graphic (embedded date 18 August, carrying SitRep 096's rapportage-18-August figures) on the 18 August row. Those figures belong on 19 August. ROW 2026-08-18 REVISED in place under the 3-day lookback with the INSP primary as source: confirmed_global 5229 -> 5126 (-1.97%), total_global 5345 -> 5242 (-1.93%), confirmed_deaths_global 2478 -> 2422 (-2.26%), recovered_global 1134 -> 1110 (-2.12%); DRC row 5,208 / 2,476 / 1,115 -> 5,105 / 2,420 / 1,091. Uganda and France unchanged. This is NOT a no-dip case: the corrected series is still cumulative non-decreasing (5042 -> 5126 -> 5229 -> 5311) because the displaced figures are re-seated one day later rather than removed. All four percent changes fall inside OBSERVED_DOWNWARD_REVISION_RANGE ("up to 100%"), so neither revision-range constant was touched.

- 2026-08-21 (run): THE +187 SINGLE-DAY RECORD DID NOT HAPPEN, and story.md was rewritten to say so. The 19 August run recorded +187 confirmed and +98 deaths on 18 August as the largest one-day increment of the series, and story.md led on whether it was a database catch-up on the pattern of the 23 July reconciliation. It was neither. It was two ordinary days folded into one row by the seating error above: +84 / +42 on 18 August and +103 / +56 on 19 August. Both sit inside the trailing fortnight's range (confirmed peaked at +117 on 12 August and +116 on 15 August; deaths ran 29 to 67). NO RECONCILIATION_EVENTS entry is added to src/generate_charts.py and none is removed, because none was ever added for 18 August. Nothing is smoothed. The lesson is narrower than it looks: a wire's day label is not a report date, and a two-day publication gap is exactly when the two come apart.

- 2026-08-22 (run): INSP PDF URL PATTERN CHANGED, and the old guess-the-filename shortcut is dead. Sitreps 093 through 097 lived at `https://insp.cd/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/NN.pdf`. SitRep 098 does not: it is at `https://insp.cd/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/SitRep_MVEBDB_098_20_08_2026_VF_98.pdf`, and `98.pdf` returns a 404 page. Separately, `web_fetch` returned an EMPTY body for every insp.cd HTML URL this run (listing page, post page, and the 404), so neither the listing nor the post page could be read without Chrome. The working recipe: load the post page in Chrome, then read the PDF URL out of the pdfemb viewer iframe — `javascript_tool` on `[...document.querySelectorAll('iframe')].map(e=>e.src)` returns a `?pdfemb-data=` query string whose base64 payload contains the real upload URL. Once you have that exact URL, `web_fetch` on the PDF itself works fine and returns the full extracted text. STANDING RECOMMENDATION FOR WEB: fold that iframe-decode step into METHODOLOGY alongside the source-preference upgrade recommended on 21 August, because filename-guessing will keep breaking.

What the feed held was aggregator traffic recycling stale counts (a radio account still quoting "over 2,300 deaths", which the tracker passed on 16 August) and shares of two Guardian pieces — a day-100 video explainer on whether this becomes the deadliest outbreak on record, and a report on frontline workers going unpaid as the outbreak spreads "exponentially". Both are narrative, neither moves a column. The unpaid-worker reporting corroborates SitRep 098's own logistics section, which records six points of control on strike for non-payment of prestataires and CREC actors unpaid for three months in Nord-Kivu and Tshopo.
- 2026-08-22 (run): declarations.csv NO CHANGE, and this time verified the way METHODOLOGY requires. Both per-country CDC Travel Health Notice pages were loaded DIRECTLY in Chrome and read, which closes out the weak verification flagged on 21 August when web_fetch returned empty bodies. [Level 4 - Ituri and Nord-Kivu](https://wwwnc.cdc.gov/travel/notices/level4/ebola-drc-provinces) and [Level 2 - DRC and Uganda](https://wwwnc.cdc.gov/travel/notices/level2/ebola-drc-uganda) both state the same three-tier structure on the page itself: Level 4 Avoid All Travel for Ituri and Nord-Kivu, Level 3 Reconsider Nonessential Travel for Haut-Uélé and Tshopo, Level 2 Practice Enhanced Precautions for the rest of DRC and for Uganda. Both pages read "Page last reviewed: August 04, 2026". Every cell in declarations.csv matches; no row edited. One cosmetic CDC inconsistency worth knowing about: the Resources block on the CDC Situation Summary links the Level 2 notice through the stale `/level1/ebola-uganda` path while labelling it Level 2. That is the same URL-reuse quirk the 31 May lesson was written about, and it is why the per-country page is read directly rather than the index or a link label.

WHO is explicit that Ervebo's protection against Bundibugyo is unknown in humans and that the trial exists to establish it. The tracker holds no vaccination column, so this is narrative only and goes to story.md.

- 2026-08-23 (run): SURVEILLANCE-INSTRUMENT CAVEAT ON THE 55-CASE DAY. The 23 August row's +56 is the lowest daily confirmed delta since 3 August (+54). Recorded here as a caution against reading it as a turn: Ituri analysed 173 samples on 22 August against 389 on 21 August while its positivity rate rose from 15.9% to 20.2%, and Ituri's total alerts fell from 763 to 274 over the same day. Fewer tests at a higher hit rate is a testing-throughput signature, not a transmission signature. No adjustment applied to the data; the tracker records reported figures on report date. Flagged for the next two or three runs.

[WHO AFRO, "Over 5200 cases recorded as Democratic Republic of the Congo crosses 100 days since Ebola outbreak declaration", 24 August 2026](https://www.afro.who.int/countries/democratic-republic-of-congo/news/over-5200-cases-recorded-democratic-republic-congocrosses100-days-ebola-outbreak-declaration). Material narrative development: WHO now calls this the DRC's fastest-growing Ebola outbreak, states transmission is outpacing control efforts, and gives an averaged ~90 confirmed cases per day across the first three months, above the equivalent rate in West Africa 2014-16 and Kivu 2018-20. Response figures cited: labs 1 to 19 with 3,000+ samples/day capacity, treatment beds under 10 to 1,300+, 900+ health facilities given IPC support, 2.5 million people reached by community engagement, contact follow-up 9% in outbreak week one to 84% as of 18 August, 260+ WHO experts deployed and 330+ tonnes of supplies delivered. Mortality: community deaths outside treatment centres are ~60% of the 260 weekly deaths recorded over the past six weeks. Ituri holds ~85% of cases and ~79% of deaths. No numeric figure in the release body (the "over 5200" is in the headline only), so nothing to seat in the CSVs from it.

- 2026-08-29 (run): RECOVERY OUTFLOW COLLAPSES ON THE 29 AUGUST ROW. SitRep 106 reports 9 patients declared cured, against 24, 30, 15, 18, 25 and 24 on the six preceding reporting days, while new confirmed cases run at 82. Derived active (live) cases therefore rise from 1,721 to 1,756, a one-day increase of 35 and the largest this month. Recorded as an observation, not a methodology decision: recovered stays cumulative and monotonic, the figure is the INSP primary's own, and a single low discharge day is as likely to be CTE throughput or reporting timing as a change in outcomes. Worth watching whether the discharge rate recovers on SitRep 107.

- 2026-08-30 (run): WHO DON616 FOUND, CORROBORATES THE 27 AUGUST ROW EXACTLY, NO REVISION DRIVEN. [Disease Outbreak News DON616](https://www.who.int/emergencies/disease-outbreak-news/item/2026-DON616), published 28 August 2026 with data as of 26 August, gives global cumulative 5,815 confirmed (DRC 5,794 including the two cases diagnosed in DRC and treated in Germany, Uganda 20, France 1) and 2,788 deaths (including Uganda 2). This tracker's 27 August row carries confirmed_global 5815 and confirmed_deaths_global 2788. Exact match on both columns from a source-preference #2 publication, and the fifth consecutive WHO artefact to confirm the D+1 seating convention (Sitreps 12, 13, 14, 15 and now DON616). DON616 also restates 60 of 151 health zones across six provinces, DRC CFR 48.1%, contact follow-up 82.3% (22,091 seen of 26,850 to follow up), Ituri 4,802 cumulative confirmed and Nord-Kivu 775 with the outbreak's highest provincial CFR at 68%, and Bas-Uélé reporting since 12 August. All consistent with the INSP-sourced rows already seated. No lookback revision applied and none warranted.
- 2026-08-30 (run): GERMANY RECOVERED DISCREPANCY CONTINUES, STILL HELD AT +2, STILL FLAGGED FOR WEB. DON616 states that as of 26 August "at least 1314 patients have recovered, including 1293 in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, 18 in Uganda, two in Germany and one in France". This tracker's 27 August row carries recovered_global 1312 = DRC 1,293 + Uganda 18 + France 1. The 2-patient gap is the same one opened on the 2026-08-17 evening run against DON615 and is unchanged in character: the two patients were diagnosed in DRC, medically evacuated to Germany, and recovered there; WHO counts them inside DRC's confirmed but outside DRC's recovered, so WHO's recovered total spans an entity this tracker does not carry as a country. Held rather than applied, on the same reasoning as before. Writing 1,314 into recovered_global without a Germany row in country_breakdown.csv would break the validator's latest-date country-sum cross-check the first day it landed on the newest row, and would make the global stop decomposing into its country rows. Consequence to state plainly: recovered_global runs 2 low against WHO from the July medevac onward, and derived active (live) cases therefore run 2 HIGH against a WHO-basis calculation. Still needs Web's call between the two closures named on 17 August (add Germany as a recovery-only entity, or state the exclusion in METHODOLOGY's "Recovered and active cases" section). Not a no-dip case; nothing revised.
- 2026-08-30 (run): IHR EMERGENCY COMMITTEE, SECOND MEETING, PUBLISHED AT LAST. The governance watch this log has carried on every run since 2026-08-18 is closed. Both artefacts from the 18 August meeting are now on the [committee page](https://www.who.int/groups/ihr-emergency-committee-regarding-the-epidemic-of-ebola-bundibugyo-virus-disease-in-the-drc-and-uganda): [temporary recommendations dated 24 August](https://www.who.int/news/item/24-08-2026-second-meeting-of-the-ihr-emergency-committee-on-the-epidemic-of-ebola-bundibugyo-virus-disease-in-the-democratic-republic-of-the-congo-temporary-recommendations) and a [meeting report dated 28 August](https://www.who.int/news/item/28-08-2026-second-meeting-of-the-ihr-emergency-committee-on-the-epidemic-of-ebola-bundibugyo-virus-disease-in-the-democratic-republic-of-the-congo---meeting-report). Substance: the DG, concurring with the Committee, determined the epidemic continues to meet the PHEIC criteria but NOT those of a pandemic emergency. This retrospectively confirms the [Actualite.cd report of 20 August](https://actualite.cd/2026/08/20/ebolardc-lurgence-internationale-maintenue-70-000-doses-dervebo-allouees) that the 2026-08-21 run recorded but correctly declined to act on. Figures the Committee put on the record, as of 12 August unless stated: true epidemic size estimated 3 to 4 times surveillance data; case-based doubling time 52 days and Rt 1.24, mortality-based doubling time 21 days and Rt 1.55, with the mortality estimate preferred because fatal outcomes are better ascertained; 55 health zones with 49 active; contact follow-up ~80% but only 13 listed contacts per case, so only ~20% of cases are found via contact follow-up and only 40% are ever linked to a chain of transmission; crude CFR ~46%; health-service use down more than 40% in some transmission areas and Ituri maternal deaths doubled since May to more than six a week; 21 labs and ~1,000 beds against an estimated 3,000 needed; WHO 274 staff deployed against a target of 455; 94 partners reporting, concentrated in Ituri, North Kivu and South Kivu; Goma and Bukavu airports closed, linked to recurrent stock-outs. The Committee warns the epidemic could exceed West Africa 2013-16 in cases and deaths without a change in trajectory, and calls for an operational reset. NONE of these figures is folded into any tracked column: they are WHO Secretariat assessments and modelled estimates on a 12 August cutoff, not surveillance counts, and this tracker's series is reported counts by report date.
- 2026-08-30 (run): DECLARATIONS.CSV UPDATED, DRC AND UGANDA. DRC `who_status` and `who_url` moved for the first time since the 17 May PHEIC determination: status now records the PHEIC maintained at the 18 August second Emergency Committee meeting, the not-a-pandemic-emergency finding, the 24 August updated temporary recommendations and the 28 August meeting report; `who_url` now points at the 24 August temporary recommendations as the most current authoritative WHO status document, following the precedent set on 2026-08-29 when Uganda's `who_url` was moved to the 27 August end-of-outbreak release. The 17 May PHEIC determination URL is preserved verbatim in the DRC `notes` field so nothing is lost. Both country `notes` fields extended. Scope point recorded on both rows: the committee and its recommendations are now titled for the DRC alone, the DRC is the only State Party in the "community transmission" tier (risk "very high" as of 14 August), and Uganda has moved into the "land borders adjoining" tier alongside Angola, Burundi, Central African Republic, Republic of Congo, Rwanda, South Sudan, Tanzania and Zambia (regional risk "high"), which carries readiness and border-health obligations rather than response obligations. Readiness self-assessment scores recorded for that tier: Rwanda 94%, Zambia 70%, South Sudan 65%, Angola 64%, Tanzania 53%, Republic of Congo 50%, Central African Republic 38%, no data for Burundi or Kenya. New in the recommendations against the 22 May set: a whole section on social measures, mass gatherings and domestic mobility (postpone mass gatherings in transmission areas, reduce crowding in food and drink establishments and nightclubs, limit motorbikes to one passenger, measures for safe school opening, 24/7 road health check points, and BVD surveillance on inland waterway vessels connecting to Kinshasa), plus immigration and customs facilitation for responders and supplies, investigation of every death occurring outside an isolation or treatment centre, and monthly implementation reporting to WHO. WHO's advice against suspending flights or denying entry to travellers from affected States Parties is restated and unchanged. No numeric column in any file moved on this.
- 2026-08-30 (run): DISSENT RECORDED. The meeting report carries an Annex documenting one Committee Member's divergent view: that the Committee's Statement extends beyond its terms of reference under IHR Articles 12, 17, 48 and 49, specifically where it addresses "operational management of the response, national response planning, resource allocation and mobilization, political or diplomatic engagement, or humanitarian and security arrangements". Logged because the Statement is an unusually direct call for an operational reset and the scope objection is part of the primary record; it does not touch any figure. Vote as recorded: all Members except one held that the epidemic continues to constitute a PHEIC but not a pandemic emergency.

- 2026-08-31 (run): READ THE 30 AND 31 AUGUST ROWS AS A PAIR, NOT AS A TREND. New confirmed cases go 82, 96, 59 across 29, 30 and 31 August, and the community share of the day's deaths goes 63.2%, 83.7%, 48.7%. Contact follow-up goes 84.4%, 82.4%, 86.3%, driven largely by Tshopo swinging from 100% to 44.0% (200 of 455) and back to 100% (455/455) in two days on a fixed denominator. A denominator that stable with a numerator that volatile is a reporting artefact, not a change in field practice, so the 30 August spike and the 31 August fall are more likely one reporting cycle split across two days than two distinct epidemiological days. Logged rather than smoothed: the series is by report date and that contract is what makes it checkable. No no-dip question arises, since every cumulative column rose on both days.

[DON616](https://www.who.int/emergencies/disease-outbreak-news/item/2026-DON616) gives, as of 26 August 2026, 5815 confirmed cases globally (5794 DRC, 20 Uganda, 1 France) and 2788 deaths. Both match this tracker's 2026-08-27 row exactly — that row seats SitRep 104, rapportage 26 août — so a source #2 publication now independently corroborates a row that previously rested on the INSP primary plus ECDC. - 2026-08-31 (run): RECOVERED BASIS DIFFERENCE AGAINST WHO DON616, HELD NOT APPLIED. DON616 states that as of 26 August "at least 1314 patients have recovered, including 1293 in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, 18 in Uganda, two in Germany and one in France". This tracker's 2026-08-27 row carries recovered 1312 = DRC 1,293 + Uganda 18 + France 1. The 2-patient gap is the two cases diagnosed in the DRC and subsequently treated in Germany, which WHO counts inside the DRC's 5,794 confirmed but breaks out separately on the recovered line; INSP's "CUMUL DES GUÉRIS" box does not include them. DECISION: hold the INSP basis and do not apply +2. Reasoning, so it can be reversed cleanly. (a) The tracker's recovered series is built country-by-country from national reporting, and adding 2 to the global without a country to carry them would break the validator's latest-date country-sum cross-check on every subsequent date. (b) Booking them under DRC would break the exact-match property against the INSP SitRep "CUMUL DES GUÉRIS" figure, which METHODOLOGY names as the tracker's main audit asset. (c) Adding Germany as a country row would give it recovered 2 against confirmed 0, since the two confirmed sit inside the DRC count, which validate.py rejects (recovered may not exceed confirmed) and which would also double-count if the confirmed were moved. CONSEQUENCE, STATED PLAINLY: from 2026-08-27 onward this tracker's recovered_global runs 2 below WHO's global recovered figure, and derived active (live) cases therefore run 2 high. On 1,767 active that is 0.1%. FLAG FOR WEB: this is a basis choice, not a data error, and it is the first time WHO has published a recovered breakdown that names a jurisdiction the tracker does not carry. If a third country ever treats DRC-diagnosed patients the gap grows and the schema question (a repatriated-cases column, or a Germany row with the confirmed moved out of DRC) becomes worth answering properly.
